# Source Registry — Latin Pastoral-Congregational Christianity

**Status:** **Approved to proceed** (self-disposed together with `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`, 2026-09-12, on Round 30's clearing verdict — 0 HIGH, 0 MEDIUM, 0 LOW, 1 disclosed COSMETIC). **The review returned 2026-09-13 was checked by `Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Returned_Review_Independent_Check_2026-09-29.md`, and the directed corrections that followed were checked by `Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Directed_Corrections_Recheck_2026-09-29.md`.** This file has been edited after that recheck; no review artifact covers those edits, and until one does the approval stands on Round 30's verdict and not on the file as it now reads. **`lpc_Decision_Log.md` is this document's single source of truth for round-by-round findings, counts, and disposition history — this line states current status only and does not restate that record.** **Row count: see the table's own final row number** — the one number this line has never been able to state reliably by hand. Built together with `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`'s own Round 14 disposition in the original 2026-09-02 pass, per `Source_Registry_Template.md` V1.0. **The V7.4 field-bibliography sweep ran once, on 2026-09-29, over the instruments the saturation statement below names, and did not close the saturation statement** — see that statement and Doc_02 §10 for the same disclosure; this disposition rests on that being knowingly partial, not complete.
**Companion to `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md`.** Boundary Status checked against `Doc_01_World_Identification_Boundaries_Orientation.md`'s stated boundary (c. 246–430 CE; Carthage and Hippo Regius, Latin-speaking Roman North Africa; Approved to proceed). Living document: append-only, no renumbering, no deletion.

**Disclosed-placement rule (stated once here rather than as row-range prose at each affected row, since the row-range form goes stale as soon as a further row is appended past it):** a handful of rows (48, 52, 60 among them) sit physically out of numeric order in the table below, because this Registry's own append-only, no-renumbering protocol above does not move an existing row to keep it adjacent to rows added later with lower numbers. This is disclosed placement, not an error. Any claim of the form "row N sits physically after rows X–Y" is a claim about the table's state at the moment it was written and goes stale the next time a row is appended between N and its own number — this Registry no longer states that range at the affected rows' own sites. To find a row's actual physical position, use the table's own row order directly; the **#** column is a row's identity, not a claim about where it physically sits.

Every source named in `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` in support of a specific claim has a corresponding row below, per the Registry's own checkpoint rule. **A stated argument for the rule's own reach, rather than left implicit for the several places it applies:** the checkpoint rule governs *sources* — primary texts, secondary scholarship, material evidence — named in support of a claim about this world. It does not reach citations to another world's own already-cleared construction record (the sibling Donatism build's Author Gravity work at Doc_02 §2, its *Deo laudes* finding at §5, `Imperial-Juridical-Christianity/Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §6's own routing note) or to the corpus map itself, which Doc_02 cites throughout as the generated census this world's own Registry is built from, not as a source this world draws on for a substantive claim. Those are citations to internal project artifacts — process and provenance, not evidence — the same category Frend's own non-citation at Doc_02 §3 discharges by argument rather than by row. Every one of these citations was independently checked against the artifact it names and found accurate.

**Confidence calibration rule, per the Template.** Confidence **A** is used only where a row's Licensed-For content was itself directly read and verified against the vendored text (or, for load-bearing claims, already independently verified in this world's own Doc_01, whose review history re-located every quotation this Registry inherits from it at source — that inheritance is stated explicitly in each such row's Verification Note rather than silently assumed). **B** is used where a specific work or locus is named accurately but was not independently re-checked against the vendored text. **C** is tied to a real author/work with no specific locus pinpointed. **D** is genre/tradition-level attribution with no specific text or author named.

| 13 | Augustine, *On Baptism, Against the Donatists* (7 books, c. 400) | P | A | Native | — | The validity of schismatic baptism/ordination; a major conduit for Cyprian's own conciliar acts, argued through at length (Doc_01 §5, §7); **and the Donatist-patrimony claim at Doc_02 §2's own Transmission History entry for Cyprian** — that the Donatists themselves appealed to Cyprian's own authority and to the 256 council's own judgments for their rebaptism doctrine | Corpus map `role: tradition`, `confidence: assigned`. I.1.2, II.3, III ch. 2, VI ch. 2 directly re-verified at source across Doc_01's own review history, with Book and Chapter loci recomputed programmatically from the vendored markup rather than accepted from citation. **Patrimony-claim grounding.** The claim's real grounding is Augustine's own continuous text at **Book III ch. 2 §2** (`v.iv.v.ii`, `div4 type="Chapter" n="2"`), which names what the Donatists bring forward "from the Council and the epistles of Cyprian, to the effect that Christ's baptism may not be given by the hands of heretics" — Augustine's own words, directly re-verified against `npnf104`, and this is what actually carries the claim. Three further elements are correctly attested but are editorial rather than Augustine's own first-person words: `div3 id="v.iv.ii"` is titled plainly **"Preface."** — NPNF's own preface to *On Baptism*, not to the *Retractations*, which is not vendored in this corpus at all; the words quoted there ("who strive to defend themselves by the authority of the most blessed bishop and martyr Cyprian") are Augustine's, from *Retractations* II.18, but reach this volume only as the NPNF editor's own quotation, introduced "Concerning it Aug. in *Retract*. Book II. c. xviii., says." Book II's own heading (`v.iv.iv`) and, by the same pattern, Books VI's (`v.iv.viii`) and VII's (`v.iv.ix`), are the **vendored edition's own editorial *argumenta*** — Book-level summaries printed as each Book's own heading, not Augustine's own words (Book II's is third-person about Augustine, "In which Augustin proves…"; Books VI's and VII's are impersonal, "In which is considered the Council of Carthage…") — the identical editorial-voice distinction this same row's companion, row 42, draws correctly for its own Zonaras quotation, and that Doc_02 §6 draws for Letter CCXI. Books VI and VII's own *contents* do examine the council's judgments in turn, which supports the claim's general shape; only their headings are the edition's own rather than the work's. The claim therefore rests on Book III ch. 2 §2's own continuous text, corroborated by the editorial framing surrounding it (the NPNF preface's own quotation of *Retractations* II.18, and Books II/VI/VII's own editorial *argumenta*), not on four loci each independently verified as Augustine's own first-person words. This is Augustine's own testimony that the Donatists made this appeal, not an independent Donatist-authored source making it in their own words — a distinction this row's own Licensed-For does not blur and Doc_02 §2 states explicitly. Corpus map entry: `cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml`. The Template's own actual rule, quoted rather than paraphrased: Boundary Status is "assessed by what the source speaks *for* — its own subject, tradition, or evidentiary target," and, on the question of whether a source may be Native to more than one world, "the question this check asks is never 'does another world already have this' — it is simply 'was this source actually used, inherited, or drawn on as part of this world's own formation, on this world's own evidence?'" Applied honestly to Letter XCIII: the census assigns it to `donatism` alone and gives it no LPC-specific role, which this row does not paper over. The case for Native here rests on the second half of the Template's own test — the letter, whatever the census's own etic assignment, is Augustine's own first-person, in-boundary account of his own change of mind, drawn on directly by this world's own Doc_01 (§7) as part of reconstructing this world's own formation history, not as evidence about Donatism's own side of anything. CF V7.4's own Step 2 scope note licenses exactly this kind of per-world assignment without requiring cross-world reconciliation: "this step governs a single world's own Registry only. Checking a candidate source against what other worlds have already claimed as native is a distinct, harder problem... intentionally not solved here." Native, on that basis. Meyer, *Theodosiani libri XVI cum Constitutionibus Sirmondianis* (Berlin: Weidmann, 1905), full text (all 16 books) — the vendored file's own two title pages read `BEROLINI APVD WEIDMANNOS MDCCCCV` and carry no edition statement, so this row names the 1905 original and asserts no edition number; the 1954 photostat reprint is a different object and would not support this row's public-domain-by-date reasoning | P | A | Native | — | The underlying imperial statute behind the Theodosian-law fine Augustine's own Letter 185 §25 cites (row 12) — not currently drawn on directly, since Doc_01's own state-power finding rests on Augustine's own primary account rather than the statute's own text, but named as a real, specific source rather than left as an unrowed background reference | **Sourcing conclusion.** The sibling Donatism build's own Decision Log records its G3 request for this edition **fully discharged**, after five failed retrieval attempts: **four** returned Mommsen's *Prolegomena in Theodosianum* (Vol. I Pars Prior), a different volume, and the second was a sourcelibrary.org file rejected on source-integrity grounds — an English paraphrase with inline glosses rather than a transcription, CC BY-SA rather than public domain, and carrying invisible zero-width Unicode characters embedded throughout. That rejection is the ground of the standing cross-world caution against sourcelibrary.org and similar "for AI systems" text intermediaries, which this world's own `Source_Acquisition_Manifest.md` carries by name. The sibling build's own Registry row 16 states both limbs ("all resolved to the same wrong volume … **or were rejected on source-integrity grounds**"); this row matches it. The correct text volume, Voluminis I Pars Posterior, was located as Internet Archive item `theodosianilibr01sirmgoog` and vendored as `cic/texts/theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt`. Boyd's 1905 translation of the ecclesiastical edicts remains a valid corroborating secondary source but is no longer that build's sole primary-text route. **Two copies of this edition, on two different rights bases.** Oxford Text Archive item 0329 (`http://purl.ox.ac.uk/ota/0329`), a legacy TEI transcription (created c. 1984) of exactly this edition, all 16 books in Latin, was supplied directly by the project lead. **It is not vendored:** OTA distributes it under a Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 3.0 licence, not a public-domain declaration, and this project's own vendoring rule (`cic/texts/README.md`) requires out-of-copyright status for anything committed to `cic/texts/`. Per the project lead's own decision it is handled as that README's own stated alternative for in-copyright material — referenced and citable, never committed. **The vendored `theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt` rests on a genuinely different basis**: a Google Books scan of the original 1905 Weidmann volume, carrying Google's own public-domain finding in the scan's retained disclaimer page and independently public domain by its 1905 date, verified here directly against that file's own provenance header. A third copy — a different edition, of unstated critical ancestry, transcribed at The Latin Library — was also supplied directly by the project lead and is **row 88** below; this row is the higher-confidence reference, row 88 the text this world had been relying on as its only committed copy. **Read against the vendored file, and not read.** Read: the constitution `XVI, 5, 21` at file lines 86853–86863 — its heading, its inscription `IDEM AAA.` (the scan prints `IDBM AAA.`; the same three Augusti) and its operative clause, `Id haereticis erroribus quoscumque constiterit vel ordinasse clericos vel suscepisse officium clericorum, denis libris auri viritim multandos esse censemus` (the scan prints `censemua`; lines 86855–86857), through to `denis libris auri proposita condemnatione multentur` at line 86863 — and, of `XVI, 5, 52`, its heading (line 87872) and the line carrying `circumcelliones argenti pondo decem` (line 87882). Not read: the constitutions' subscriptions and variant apparatus, any other title of Book XVI, and the rest of the file's 94,021 lines. The file's inscription line does not name the emperor; the name Theodosius comes from §25. **The provision behind Augustine's own Letter 185 §25 is *CTh* XVI.5.21, not XVI.5.52, and the two are easy to collapse into each other.** §25 (`div2 v.vi`, "The Correction of the Donatists") records the proposal "to take the law which **Theodosius, of pious memory**, enacted **generally against heretics of all kinds**, to the effect that **any heretical bishop or clergyman, being found in any place, should be fined ten pounds of gold**, and confirm it in more express terms against the Donatists." *CTh* XVI.5.21, headed `XVI, 5, 21 (392 lun. 15)` at line 86853 of the vendored file (Book XVI, title 5; the scan prints `lun.` for the month abbreviation) and reading `denis libris auri viritim` at line 86856, penalizes anyone who ordains or accepts clerical office in heretical error — heretical clergy generally, ten pounds of gold each, in a constitution §25 attributes to Theodosius. "Generally against heretics of all kinds" and "any heretical bishop or clergyman" map clause for clause onto its scope. *CTh* XVI.5.52, headed `XVI, 5, 52 (412 lan. 30>` at line 87872 and carrying `circumcelliones argenti pondo decem` at line 87882, verbatim, is a **graduated Donatist schedule**, every rank of it assessed in gold (`auri pondo`: illustres fifty pounds, spectabiles forty, senators thirty, clarissimi twenty, sacerdotales thirty, principales twenty, decuriones, negotiatores and plebei five each, lines 87878–87882), except the circumcelliones, who are assessed in silver (`argenti pondo decem`, line 87882): it discharges a gap belonging to the *Donatism* build, not this one, and reading it as this world's is the borrowing-from-the-wrong-world error this project's own history names. **Chronology, stated so the distinction cannot collapse again:** XVI.5.21 is of 392; the council §25 narrates is dated 401 by NPNF's own endnote (n. 2521: "held June 26 (more correctly, probably June 15th or 16th), 401"), not by Augustine's text; XVI.5.52 is of 412 — eleven years after the events §25 recounts, under different emperors. A different provision, a different emperor and date, and a schedule by rank where XVI.5.21 fixes one sum for all. Identified against both files directly. **A search caution for whoever re-checks §25:** the vendored `npnf104_augustine-anti-manichaean-anti-donatist.xml` breaks the phrase across a line as `ten\npounds of gold`, so a strict search misses §25's own instance and lands instead on a sentence from *Answer to the Letters of Petilian* Book II (`div2 v.v`). Quote §25 from `div2 v.vi`. **A normalization note:** XVI.5.52's heading is given here as the scan prints it, `(412 lan. 30>`; the sibling build's records normalize it to "412 Ian. 30", and that normalization is theirs, not a reading taken here. **Held in the shared corpus as `cic/texts/theodosianus-16_mommsen-meyer1905.txt`, and so directly usable by this document**, the same position row 37 records for *CIL* VIII. **Confidence is A.** The calibration rule gives A where a row's Licensed-For content was itself directly read and verified against the vendored text. This row's Licensed-For is confined to the imperial statute behind the fine Letter 185 §25 cites; that statute, *CTh* XVI.5.21, was read against the vendored file at its heading (line 86853), its operative clause (lines 86855–86857, `denis libris auri viritim` at line 86856) and its closing penalty clause (line 86863), and mapped clause for clause to §25's description of it. The one inference, that §25's Theodosius is the emperor of that constitution because the file's inscription line does not name him, is stated above; what is not read is stated above and lies outside the Licensed-For. Source: the Donatism build's own Registry, row 16, and Manifest, G3 | — | 2026-09-01, `lpc` build thread | Donatism Registry / 2026-09-01 |

Lancel's own Sources Chrétiennes edition is in copyright and remains consultation-only. **The *Gesta* themselves are separable from Lancel's edition of them, and are available.** Lancel's edition is modern and in copyright; the *Gesta* are an ancient text whose earlier printings carry their own rights position, and conflating the two conceals that. A full Migne *Patrologia Latina* XI printing of the *Gesta Collationis Carthaginiensis* was located and vendored by the sibling Donatism build as `cic/texts/pl11-zeno-optatus-collatio-carthaginiensis_migne.txt` (Internet Archive item `patrologiaecur11mign`), public domain on the same by-date basis this corpus already accepts for its other Migne PL volumes — correcting — not closing — that build's own Registry row 14 (which withdrew its erroneous rights finding but deliberately held Confidence at B, its text still unread in full), which had recorded the *Gesta* as "confirmed unavailable in the public domain" on a determination made without working outbound network access. **Content independently verified, directly against that file rather than taken from the sibling build's own account of it:** Augustine is a named disputant. **Two different kinds of evidence, distinguished here rather than run together:** (i) he is listed among the seven Catholic *actores* in the volume's delegate roster — the `ACTORES VII. / EX PARTE CATHOLICORUM` table, its heading at file line 113068 and Augustine's own entry, `Augustinus  Uipporegiensis.`, third in the Catholic column, at file line 113076 (both line numbers re-derived against the file, and each is found by its own text), in the *Gesta* section's preliminary matter, not in the acts themselves — alongside Aurelius of Carthage, Alypius of Thagaste and Possidius of Calama; and (ii) he speaks in his own recorded voice in the numbered acts. **Quotation discipline:** this scan's OCR is poor and its own provenance header warns in terms against verbatim reliance without visual cross-check, so readings are given as normalizations, not quotations. The roster prints `Augustinus Uipporegiensis` (the scan's routine H/U substitution); normalized, *Augustinus Hipporegiensis*. The speech formula is garbled differently at every instance (`Eccleshv catholicai dixil`, `Ecclesia; catholicm dixit`, `Ecclesios caUiolicai dixit`); normalized, *Augustinus episcopus Ecclesiae catholicae dixit*. **Count:** an OCR-tolerant line-initial scan across the *Gesta* span returns **fourteen** numbered lines that begin `N. Augustinus` — acts 50, 53, 98, 158, 160, 162, 187, 189, 201, 206, 257, 265, 267, 272. **Thirteen of them are speeches**, each carrying the formula normalized above (*Augustinus episcopus Ecclesiae catholicae dixit*); the fourteenth, act 158, is not a speech (see below). The number of acts in which Augustine speaks is therefore a floor of thirteen: mid-line instances and heavier garblings are not counted, and any restatement of this figure elsewhere should carry the floor with it rather than assert thirteen flat. **A locating caution:** the *Gesta*'s act numbering is not unique across this volume — act 158 sits several thousand lines before act 50 — so these fourteen lines span more than one separately-numbered sequence and a bare act number does not by itself locate a passage; cite by file line as well when drawing on one. **Act 158 is a subscription to a mandate, not a debate speech.** Its line (file line 121764, `158.  Augustinus  episcop`) stops after the name and carries none of the formula the other thirteen share, because the act runs straight on into the subscription formula with no other speaker between: line 121765 is garbled and ends `priEsente` (*praesente*), line 121767 names the tribune and notary (`Marcdliuo`, *Marcellino*), and line 121768 reads `mendalum ·usccpi fll nibacripsj`, i.e. *mandatum suscepi et subscripsi*. The neighbouring acts, at lines 121756 and 121790 (their numbers and words are garbled in the scan), read as Petilianus *dixit*. Act 158 does not support a claim about the recorded speech form. **What this does not change:** this row's own Licensed-For position stands unaltered — Doc_02 §2's "post-411 Conference" phrase still rests on the Conference's own ordinary, undisputed date, not on any reading of the acts, so no claim currently in Doc_02 depends on this correction. **What it opens:** a live, recorded primary route to Augustine's own voice at the 411 Conference exists **in this project's shared vendored corpus, `cic/texts/pl11-zeno-optatus-collatio-carthaginiensis_migne.txt`, and is assigned to this world's own corpus-map entry** (`cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml`, `role: context`, `confidence: provisional`), bearing directly on this world's own account of his institutional confidence in that period — **available to Doc_04. Doc_04 draws no conclusion from it and relies on one narrow fact from it: act 158 is Augustine's subscription to the mandate, not a debate speech (Doc_04 §7, item 6).** Not read beyond the role-and-speech verification described above. **Sweep for this same defect shape across the rest of the table, so that a correction made at one row is not left unchecked at its siblings.** The shape is: a modern in-copyright critical edition marked "not a vendoring candidate," where an earlier printing of the same underlying ancient text carries a different rights position that was never checked. Eight Type-P rows carry that phrase. Rows 64 (Labrousse) and 67 (Mutzenbecher) already name their public-domain alternatives — Ziwsa's CSEL 26 and Knöll's CSEL 36 — the latter the direct precedent for this row. Rows 49 and 50 (Divjak, Dolbeau) are genuinely clean: newly discovered letters and sermons with no earlier printing to have. **Two residuals, named rather than left to be rediscovered later. Row 58** (*Sancti Cypriani episcopi opera*, CCSL 3/3A/3B–3D) is the stronger instance: its public-domain predecessor, Hartel's CSEL 3, is **already vendored** at row 191, so the alternative this shape asks about is not merely locatable but already in hand and already rowed. **Row 59** (Munier, *Concilia Africae*, CCSL 149) has the same structure, with no earlier printing of the African conciliar corpus named or ruled out. Nothing turns on it today — row 26, the claim it licenses, already rests on secondary characterization — so it is disclosed here as a lead rather than acted on | — | 2026-09-02, `lpc` build thread | WebSearch / 2026-09-02 |

Site-navigation chrome and Google's own standard public-domain usage notice were stripped, doubled inter-word spacing normalized, nothing else altered. **OCR quality assessed directly, not assumed:** sampled at multiple points spanning the full document (opening treatise, mid-document, the Pars I/II title-page transition, and the closing lines of Epistle 81) — the running Latin text reads coherently and without truncation throughout, including at the very end; the apparatus criticus shows meaningfully more degradation (garbled manuscript sigla, stray characters), disclosed in the file's own header rather than treated as pristine — a real but narrower defect than the OCR failure that caused a Tischendorf scan to be rejected elsewhere in this project's own shared corpus this same week, where the title page itself was unreadable; here both Pars I's and Pars II's own title pages read correctly. Original-language witness (Latin) — second-witness caveat applies per `cic/texts/INTAKE.md`: never primary evidence for a Representative on its own, only for cross-checking a specific reading against the vendored English translation. **Confidence C on OCR grounds:** the calibration rule above ties C to a real author or work with no locus pinpointed, and the works of this row are named; C is given here for the OCR quality assessed above, a ground the rule's text does not name. ## Priority second-opinion review flags

**Two governing texts state this trigger differently, and this Registry applies both rather than picking one silently.** `Source_Registry_Template.md`'s own re-keyed trigger: a source discovered from the builder's own prior knowledge rather than independently re-collated or web-verified, licensing a load-bearing, vivid, specific claim. CF V7.4's own Step 2 text: "Flag entries resting at Confidence C or below that are intended to support a vivid, specific claim." The two diverge — the Template's own trigger does not reach a Confidence-C row that is not builder-prior-knowledge (e.g., row 8 below), and CF V7.4's own literal text does not exempt a row merely because it was independently checked once, if what it is now asked to support is vivid and specific. **Whether the Template's own re-keyed trigger actually supersedes CF V7.4's literal text on this point, or the two stand side by side, is a reading of two governing documents this build thread does not have editing authority over (per `cic-build-cycle`'s own *Coach verification* section) and does not resolve here — flagged for a coach pass rather than settled by this document's own assertion.** Applying both triggers, as this Registry does below, is the safe course regardless of which reading is eventually confirmed.

**The rule, stated once rather than maintained as a per-row list (an enumerated version drifts out of step with the table it describes — the same remedy already applied to the Manifest's own §3 list):** a row is flagged for priority second-opinion review when it is Confidence C or below **and** it licenses a claim this document actually makes (not merely a non-empty Licensed-For field, which can state a negative). This is a reading of CF V7.4's own literal trigger this build thread applies as its own working rule, not a governance ruling — the Template's own re-keyed trigger (discovery-channel/evidentiary-weight based) states a narrower test, and whether the Template's own trigger supersedes CF V7.4's literal text or the two stand side by side is a reading of two governing documents this build thread does not have editing authority over; both are applied, and the divergence is flagged for a coach pass rather than settled here. Checking any given row against this rule is a lookup against its own Confidence and Licensed-For columns, not against a list maintained separately from them. Most rows' own Verification Notes state plainly whether they are flagged; where a row is silent on the point (a real gap, not a claim that it is exempt), the rule above still applies and governs. Two cautions this rule's own application has produced. A row's Licensed-For can be widened to carry a vivid claim at Confidence C without this section being re-run, moving the row across the rule while this section's own text stays unchanged — row 42, since reverted. And a Licensed-For can need narrowing once the claim it was carrying turns out to rest on something else: row 65's was narrowed when Doc_02's own 411-date claim was found to rest on the event's ordinary datability rather than on that row's own unread edition.

**Any row whose Boundary Status is Excluded is outside the trigger's own reach in either reading** (stated as a rule rather than an enumerated list, since any such list goes stale as rows are reclassified and the enumeration stops matching the table's own current Excluded set. **No replacement count is given here either.** To see which rows are Excluded at any given moment, read the table's own Boundary Status column directly.), since neither governing text licenses a claim through an excluded source; their own Verification Notes are the record of what was checked to exclude them. Rows 36 and 37's own general identification rely in part on the sibling Donatism build's own independent verification, disclosed as such rather than presented as this document's own check.

## Discovery methodology note

No `records/lpc/search_record/` was kept as searches happened during this build. The Discovery column above reflects, for primary sources, the existing `cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml` census (itself compiled by `cic/engine/corpus_map_merge.py` from `cic/corpus-map/_staging/` source-volume files, not by this build thread) and, for load-bearing claims, Doc_01's own review history's direct re-verification against the vendored texts. For secondary scholarship and unvendored acquisition candidates, the Discovery column records the specific WebSearch queries run (real results, not invented) where a search was actually run, and `builder-prior-knowledge / field knowledge` where a work is named from general disciplinary familiarity without an independent check. This is disclosed rather than presented as a contemporaneous per-search log; it is dated to when each entry was written during this drafting pass.

## Saturation statement

The corpus-map census figures (raw entries, `tradition`-role entries, distinct titles, and the comparison with the next-largest atlas file) are given, with their date and counting method, at `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §1's opening paragraph, the one place they are stated. The census changes as sources are vendored; a later count is recomputed by the method stated there.

**The ten-item relative-recall test and PRESS question, per CF V7.4's own Doc_02 review requirement, ran once per review pass for the first fourteen passes. No later pass has run either instrument** — every later pass instead verified the prior pass's own fix work against this world's own source-integration revision, with a cold whole-document read added from the eighteenth pass on, and none repeated the recall test or the PRESS question in doing so. The full pass-by-pass accounting — each pass's own instrument set, recall score, and the rows it dispositioned — is recorded in `lpc_Decision_Log.md` and in the review artifacts (`Review-Artifacts/Doc02_Round1_Review.md` through `Doc02_Round14_Review.md`), not restated here. In summary: recall stayed at 0/10 across all fourteen passes except the second (3/10, a re-finding of the first pass's own PRESS dispositions rather than new coverage).

**Independent verification credited across the passes that ran the test, not only imported as a claim.** The first pass (`Doc02_Round1_Review.md`) independently WebSearch-verified rows **31** (Lancel), **32** (Burns, *Cyprian the Bishop*), **33** (Burns & Jensen), **34** (Fahey), and **35** (Rebillard) as bibliographically accurate. Rows 34 and 35 continue to state that they are not independently checked or bibliographically re-verified via WebSearch and remain flagged. Rows 32 and 33 no longer carry that flag: each was independently WebSearch-re-verified in its own right and the flag resolved.

**Field-bibliography sweep.**

**Method.** The sweep opened the reference instruments below. Three of them were read by content probe, counting names in the OCR text: Bardenhewer's *Geschichte der altkirchlichen Literatur* Band 4 (Augustine, the Donatists, Petilian), Band 3 (Optatus, Tyconius) and Band 2 (Cyprian). Of the other nine, seven had their title pages read from the OCR (Bardenhewer's *Patrology*, the 1922 Schanz-Hosius-Krüger, Teuffel, Ebert, Tixeront's *Handbook of Patrology*, Duchesne and Smith and Wace); for the other two, the 1896 Schanz and Tixeront's *Histoire des dogmes*, the title page was not confirmed from the OCR and the volume statement rests on the host record, as the queue rows in `Build/worlds/_cross-world/download-queue-seed.yaml` state. None was read through. The instruments: Bardenhewer, *Geschichte der altkirchlichen Literatur*, Band 2 (2nd ed., 1914), Band 3 (1912) and Band 4 (1924), and his *Patrology* (Shahan's English translation of 1908); Schanz-Hosius-Krüger, *Geschichte der römischen Literatur*, Teil 3 (1922) and the 1896 edition; Teuffel, *History of Roman Literature* (1891), whose scan is Vol. I, *The Republican Period*; Ebert, *Allgemeine Geschichte der Literatur des Mittelalters im Abendlande*, Band 1 (1874); Tixeront, *Handbook of Patrology* (1923) and *Histoire des dogmes*, tome 1 (1906); Duchesne, *Early History of the Christian Church*, whose scan is Vol. II, *The Fourth Century*; and Smith and Wace, *Dictionary of Christian Biography*, vol. 1 (1877). Monceaux's *Histoire littéraire de l'Afrique chrétienne*, tomes I–VI, are vendored (tomes I–III have rows 206–208; tomes IV–VI are vendored and have no row of their own). The OpenGreekAndLatin `csel-dev` TEI repository was probed by path across the textgroups stoa0001 to stoa0420, each id tried bare and with an `a` and a `b` suffix (1,260 ids). It holds Augustine (41 files), Cyprian (`data/stoa0104a`, 15 works), Optatus (`data/stoa0215a`: the *libri VII* and the *Appendix decem monumentorum*), Arnobius, Commodian, Lactantius, Minucius Felix, Tertullian and pseudo-Tertullian, among others. The Cyprian and Optatus files are vendored as rows 249–265. No Tyconius textgroup was found under any of those ids.

**Result.** The sweep checked Cyprian, Augustine's pastoral and congregational works, the Donatist sources and other North African sources against what the Library holds. It vendored 25 files, rows 218–242: seven `csel-dev` TEI files of Augustine (*De fide et operibus*, *De agone christiano*, *De bono coniugali*, *De sancta virginitate*, *De mendacio*, *Contra mendacium*, *De opere monachorum*, rows 218–224) and the CSEL 41 volume scan that prints them (row 225); Krueger's *De catechizandis rudibus* (row 226); the Maurist sermons and tractates in Migne (rows 227–228); Morin's *Sermones Guelferbytani* and Caillau's *Sermones inediti* (rows 229–230); two collections of martyr acts (rows 231–232, with their Scillitan and Perpetua acts as rows 245–248); and scholarship and reference works (rows 233–242). It verified five further volumes and left them unvendored (Migne's Maurist tomes VI and IX, CSEL 25/1, Victor of Vita in CSEL 7, and the Mauretanian part of CIL VIII), and opened twelve reference works it did not vendor. The reason for each is in `Build/worlds/_cross-world/download-queue-seed.yaml`, in the row's `hold_note`. The `csel-dev` files vendored for this world number 24: the seven Augustine files above, the fifteen Cyprian files (rows 249–263) and the two Optatus files (rows 264–265). Seven works of Petschenig's CSEL 51–53 volume, which was already vendored, are also registered (rows 266–272). The other `csel-dev` Augustine files were not vendored; among them the Confessions, the Retractationes, the Epistulae of CSEL 34/2 and the anti-Donatist works of Petschenig's CSEL 51–53 are vendored from the printed volumes (rows 197, 209, 195 and 214–217). The totals of works enumerated in each category, and the number of works found only in copyright, are not recorded in a file this Registry can cite, and they are not stated here. Works found only in copyright stay consultation-only rows (for example CCSL 3, row 58, and Mandouze's *Prosopographie*, row 62).

**What the sweep did not reach.** Bardenhewer Band 1 (its text could not be retrieved) and Band 5 (1932, later than the Library's date rule) were not read. The Teuffel volumes on the imperial period, where the Christian Latin authors stand, and Duchesne Vol. I were not opened, because the scans opened are the volumes named above. `csel-dev` was probed only by path, so a work not named in the probe is unchecked, and its older branch of whole-volume XML could not be read. The works inside the `Anonymous` textgroup stoa0329 and the ids above stoa0420 were not probed. ANF10's bibliographic synopsis, vendored, was not read in this pass. The CSEL series volume list and the Gallica and HathiTrust copies of Monceaux were not checked.

**What the sandbox blocked.** These hosts were unreachable: ccel.org, tertullian.org, newadvent.org, documentacatholicaomnia.eu, gallica.bnf.fr, thelatinlibrary.com, csel.at, perseus.tufts.edu, zenodo.org, patristica.net, hdl.handle.net, jsdelivr and Google Books. babel.hathitrust.org, github.com pages, codeload and api.github.com returned 403. That stopped a `csel-dev` directory listing, the old whole-volume XML, Gallica and HathiTrust copies of Monceaux, and the csel.sbg.ac.at volume list. Raw files on `raw.githubusercontent.com` and the archive.org items were reachable.

**Saturation basis.** The census this Registry is built against is the corpus-map atlas file for this world, `cic/corpus-map/latin-pastoral-congregational-christianity.yaml`. As counted against corpus-map commit `3da8738f2`, the file holds 173 entries: 153 `role: tradition` (127 `assigned`, 26 `provisional`) and 20 `role: context`. The count at the Registry's first rows was 73 works (65 `assigned`, 8 `provisional`), parsed directly, not estimated; the count after the vendoring recorded at rows 191–212 was 99 works (87 `assigned`, 12 `provisional`). The later entries are second-witness and sweep files, accounted for at `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §1, which states the current count and its method.

This Registry is **not** closed with a full Construction Framework V7.4 saturation statement. What has been done is named above and in the passes before it, and what remains — the instruments and volumes named under "What the sweep did not reach", and the per-category totals — is disclosed as a limit at `Doc_02_Source_Ecology.md` §9, consistent with the same disclosure the sibling Donatism build's own Registry makes for itself.
